If the selected run's project group is collapsed, the row won't exist in the DOM, so the lookup fails and returns without marking it attempted, meaning it'll retry on the next render—but since the user says the row lands "a bit higher" rather than being missing entirely, the row must exist here. Now I'm sketching out the actual fix.

I'm designing a scroll manager that tracks scroll position and selected run per surface, storing state in a map keyed by surface type. Since the existing tests check for specific string tokens in the source rather than behavior, I'll need to update those assertions to match my new implementation while keeping them meaningful. Writing scroll manager class...

scrollIntoView instead? Because it doesn't handle the actual timing issue -- the drawer's enter transition and viewport resizing mean I need to keep re-centering until the layout settles, using an effect that watches for stability before stopping.

Otherwise, for mobile with a selected run, I try centering that row in the viewport, retrying on later renders if it's not mounted yet. Since the drawer animates in and the viewport keeps resizing for several frames, I need to account for the pre-settle layout so the row doesn't end up mis-centered once things stabilize.

I'm planning a re-centering loop that keeps correcting position via requestAnimationFrame for about 400ms, capped by frame count or elapsed time, and cancels as soon as the user interacts with the list via pointerdown, wheel, or touchstart so we don't fight their scrolling. The programmatic scroll will still trigger the existing save listener, which is fine since it saves against the current selected run.

A cleaner alternative occurs to me: use a ResizeObserver on the viewport and its scroll content element to re-center whenever the size changes, stopping on the first user interaction or unmount, supplemented with a couple of requestAnimationFrame passes to catch late reflows like image loads. I'm weighing whether this needs a timeout cutoff too, since without one, something like a keyboard opening and resizing a drawer much later could trigger an unwanted re-center.

Actually, I want to check if this is over-engineering the fix. The stale saved-scroll bug alone explains the "often a bit off" symptom as a clear logic issue, while the timing bug may not even be the real cause -- so I should look at the Sheet component's mounting behavior, specifically whether it renders with zero height initially due to portal transitions before layout effects run.

I'm ruling out other explanations too—ancestor scrollIntoView effects on page scroll, hydration timing, sticky headers overlapping the row—none of these quite account for the row landing slightly above center. I suspect there's still a more specific cause I haven't pinned down yet for why the target ends up a bit higher than expected.

Rereading the user's phrasing "ends up a little bit higher up, I need to scroll a bit" — that means scrollTop is too large, pushing the row above the visible area, so they scroll up to reach it. That points to restoring a stale saved scrollTop from when a lower run was selected, so the current run (positioned higher in the list) ends up hidden above the viewport.

I should also check whether the list reorders by recency, which would make the old scroll position even more wrong. I'll confirm Bug A as the fix, and consider hardening the centering logic by dropping ancestor scrollIntoView calls and clamping values explicitly, though I want to keep any extra correction pass minimal rather than building a full observer system — checking whether row content like icons could cause layout shift that would justify it.

Given the drawer opens well after fonts load, layout shift isn't really a concern, so I'll avoid speculative complexity and just fix the staleness bug with precise clamped math. I'm also considering edge cases: if the selected run's project group is collapsed the row won't exist so scrolling is simply skipped, and since `scrollRestoreAttemptedRef` is per-mount while the mobile drawer remounts each time it opens but the desktop sidebar stays mounted, the desktop restore path only fires once at initial mount, which is fine.

For the case where a user taps a run to close the mobile drawer, the timing of unmount versus the effect's closure over `selectedRunId` matters — if cleanup captures the old id, the next open will see a differing selectedRunId and center; if it captures the new id, the next open will just restore to that position. Either outcome seems acceptable.

But actually the real problem is timeline: user opens drawer centered on A, scrolls down, taps B while B is off-center, drawer closes and saves the current scrollTop tied to B, then reopening restores that same scrollTop instead of centering on B — this exactly explains the "not centered, a bit higher up" bug, since the tap location gets preserved rather than recentered.

The cleanest fix is: on mobile, whenever there's a selected run to show, always center it on open, and only fall back to the remembered scroll offset when there's no selection to center on — this honors the stated intent while keeping the remembered-scroll behavior for desktop and the no-selection case.

But I need to be careful: if the selected row isn't rendered yet, like from a collapsed group or async loading, permanently falling back to the saved offset is wrong — on mobile with a selectedRunId but no matching row, I should restore the saved position without marking the attempt as fully complete, so it can retry once the row appears.

Repeatedly restoring the saved offset would fight the user if they scroll manually, so I want a cleaner approach: track whether the user has taken control of scrolling via the scroll listener, and only keep attempting to center the selected run while that hasn't happened. This way late-arriving rows still get centered, but a user's manual scroll is respected. I'm setting up a ref to track this settled state.

I compare scrollTop to the target to detect real user scrolls, and use a layout effect that keeps trying to center the row until it succeeds once, then marks settled so it won't fight further user scrolling. If the row disappears I fall back to restoring the saved offset without marking settled.

The cleanup still saves scroll position on unmount, and I'm treating a null initial ref value as "no programmatic scroll yet" so any early scroll counts as user-driven, which seems fine given momentum scrolling keeps settled=true once triggered.
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/components/home/ConversationSidebar.tsx",
  "old_string": "const conversationSidebarScrollManager = new ConversationSidebarScrollManager();",
  "new_string": "const conversationSidebarScrollManager = new ConversationSidebarScrollManager();\n\nfunction findConversationRunRow(viewport: HTMLElement, runId: string) {\n  return Array.from(viewport.querySelectorAll<HTMLElement>(\"[data-conversation-run-id]\"))\n    .find((row) => row.dataset.conversationRunId === runId) ?? null;\n}\n\n/**\n * `scrollIntoView({ block: \"center\" })` also scrolls every scrollable ancestor\n * (the drawer, the document) and rounds against the row's animated position, so\n * the offset is computed here and written to the list viewport alone.\n */\nfunction centeredScrollTopForRow(viewport: HTMLElement, row: HTMLElement) {\n  const maxScrollTop = Math.max(0, viewport.scrollHeight - viewport.clientHeight);\n  const rowTop = row.getBoundingClientRect().top - viewport.getBoundingClientRect().top + viewport.scrollTop;\n  const centered = rowTop - (viewport.clientHeight - row.offsetHeight) / 2;\n  return Math.min(Math.max(centered, 0), maxScrollTop);\n}"
}
```

```text
import fs from "fs";
import path from "path";
import { expect, test } from "vitest";

const readSource = (relativePath: string) => fs.readFileSync(path.resolve(process.cwd(), relativePath), "utf8");

test("the compact conversation surface opens the existing mobile conversation list on a right swipe", () => {
  const conversationMainSource = readSource("src/components/home/ConversationMain.tsx");
  const conversationSidebarSource = readSource("src/components/home/ConversationSidebar.tsx");
  const homeAppSource = readSource("src/interface/home/HomeApp.tsx");

  expect(conversationMainSource).toContain("onOpenMobileConversationList: () => void;");
  expect(conversationMainSource).toContain("mobileConversationSwipeManager.start");
  expect(conversationMainSource).toContain("mobileConversationSwipeManager.move");
  expect(conversationMainSource).toContain("onPointerMove={handleConversationPointerMove}");
  expect(conversationMainSource).toContain("mobileConversationSwipeManager.finish");
  expect(conversationMainSource).toContain("onPointerCancel={handleConversationPointerCancel}");
  expect(conversationMainSource).toContain('viewportClassName="touch-pan-y touch-pinch-zoom lg:touch-auto"');
  expect(conversationMainSource).not.toContain("setPointerCapture");
  expect(homeAppSource).toContain("onOpenMobileConversationList={handleOpenMobileConversationList}");
  expect(homeAppSource).toContain("setMobileNavOpen(true)");

  expect(conversationSidebarSource).toContain("mobileConversationSwipeManager.start");
  expect(conversationSidebarSource).toContain('mobileConversationSwipeManager.move(event, "left")');
  expect(conversationSidebarSource).toContain("onPointerMove={handleSidebarPointerMove}");
  expect(conversationSidebarSource).toContain('mobileConversationSwipeManager.finish(event, "left")');
  expect(conversationSidebarSource).toContain("onPointerCancel={handleSidebarPointerCancel}");
  expect(conversationSidebarSource).toContain('viewportClassName="touch-pan-y touch-pinch-zoom lg:touch-auto"');
  expect(conversationSidebarSource).not.toContain("setPointerCapture");
});
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/components/home/conversation-sidebar-scroll.ts",
  "content": "export type ConversationSidebarRowMetrics = {\n  /** Full scrollable height of the conversation list. */\n  scrollHeight: number;\n  /** Visible height of the list viewport. */\n  clientHeight: number;\n  /** Row offset measured from the top of the scrollable content. */\n  rowTop: number;\n  /** Row height, so a tall row centres on its middle rather than its top edge. */\n  rowHeight: number;\n};\n\nexport function clampScrollTop(scrollTop: number, scrollHeight: number, clientHeight: number) {\n  return Math.min(Math.max(scrollTop, 0), Math.max(0, scrollHeight - clientHeight));\n}\n\n/**\n * Offset that puts a conversation row in the vertical middle of the list\n * viewport, clamped to the scrollable range so rows near either end settle\n * flush instead of asking for an offset the viewport cannot reach.\n */\nexport function centeredScrollTop({ scrollHeight, clientHeight, rowTop, rowHeight }: ConversationSidebarRowMetrics) {\n  return clampScrollTop(rowTop - (clientHeight - rowHeight) / 2, scrollHeight, clientHeight);\n}\n"
}
```

```json
  "file_path": "/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/components/home/ConversationSidebar.tsx",
  "old_string": "/**\n * `scrollIntoView({ block: \"center\" })` also scrolls every scrollable ancestor\n * (the drawer, the document) and rounds against the row's animated position, so\n * the offset is computed here and written to the list viewport alone.\n */\nfunction centeredScrollTopForRow(viewport: HTMLElement, row: HTMLElement) {\n  const maxScrollTop = Math.max(0, viewport.scrollHeight - viewport.clientHeight);\n  const rowTop = row.getBoundingClientRect().top - viewport.getBoundingClientRect().top + viewport.scrollTop;\n  const centered = rowTop - (viewport.clientHeight - row.offsetHeight) / 2;\n  return Math.min(Math.max(centered, 0), maxScrollTop);\n}",
  "new_string": "/**\n * `scrollIntoView({ block: \"center\" })` also scrolls every scrollable ancestor\n * (the drawer, the document) and rounds against the row's animated position, so\n * the offset is measured here and written to the list viewport alone.\n */\nfunction centeredScrollTopForRow(viewport: HTMLElement, row: HTMLElement) {\n  return centeredScrollTop({\n    scrollHeight: viewport.scrollHeight,\n    clientHeight: viewport.clientHeight,\n    rowTop: row.getBoundingClientRect().top - viewport.getBoundingClientRect().top + viewport.scrollTop,\n    rowHeight: row.offsetHeight,\n  });\n}"
}
```

```json
  "content": "import { expect, test } from \"vitest\";\n\nimport { centeredScrollTop, clampScrollTop } from \"@/components/home/conversation-sidebar-scroll\";\n\ntest(\"a selected row lands in the vertical middle of the list viewport\", () => {\n  const scrollTop = centeredScrollTop({\n    scrollHeight: 2000,\n    clientHeight: 600,\n    rowTop: 1000,\n    rowHeight: 40,\n  });\n\n  expect(scrollTop).toBe(720);\n  // The row centre sits on the viewport centre, not above it.\n  expect(1000 + 40 / 2 - scrollTop).toBe(600 / 2);\n});\n\ntest(\"rows near either end settle flush instead of overscrolling\", () => {\n  expect(centeredScrollTop({ scrollHeight: 2000, clientHeight: 600, rowTop: 20, rowHeight: 40 })).toBe(0);\n  expect(centeredScrollTop({ scrollHeight: 2000, clientHeight: 600, rowTop: 1900, rowHeight: 40 })).toBe(1400);\n});\n\ntest(\"a list shorter than its viewport stays at the top\", () => {\n  expect(centeredScrollTop({ scrollHeight: 300, clientHeight: 600, rowTop: 200, rowHeight: 40 })).toBe(0);\n});\n\ntest(\"remembered offsets are clamped to the scrollable range\", () => {\n  expect(clampScrollTop(5000, 2000, 600)).toBe(1400);\n  expect(clampScrollTop(-20, 2000, 600)).toBe(0);\n  expect(clampScrollTop(300, 2000, 600)).toBe(300);\n});\n"
}
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-26T10:30:54.223Z

Typechecking passes now, so I'm double-checking the mobile flow where there's no selected run — verifying that centering, settling, and the programmatic scroll ref correctly avoid triggering the "user scrolled" logic. I want to confirm the ordering between the layout effect that centers on mount and the scroll listener effect to make sure they don't race.

Even if the listener attaches slightly after the programmatic write fires, missing that one scroll event is harmless since cleanup saves position on unmount anyway. I also want to make sure a tolerance of 1px handles iOS momentum scroll's fractional values, and that no spurious scroll event could mark things "settled" before the centering runs. Now I'm rereading the full effect to confirm it all holds together.

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-26T10:31:18.549Z

I'm double-checking that the mobile drawer's height calculation is reliable, confirming that clientHeight is accurate at layout time given the fixed positioning and flex column structure of the sidebar.

Still, there's a lingering risk: if content like runner controls loads lazily and changes the viewport height after mount, the centered scroll position could drift, and my settled-ref guard would prevent correction. Adding a ResizeObserver to re-center on height changes seems risky though, since it could cause unwanted jumps when the keyboard opens during search input focus — so I'm leaning toward trusting that the ordering fix addresses the main reported issue rather than adding this extra safeguard.

A safer middle ground occurs to me: scheduling a single re-center on the next animation frame after opening, which would catch any late layout settling without re-triggering on later interactions. I'll add this bounded rAF pass, cancelled either on cleanup or once the user scrolls, making sure it still runs even though the settled flag is set immediately.

That's actually fine within useLayoutEffect. But I realize the row element could be detached by the time the frame fires, which would make getBoundingClientRect return zeros and cause a jump to the top — I need to guard with `selectedRow.isConnected`. I'm also weighing whether this rAF re-center is even necessary, or if it's just dead code adding risk, versus genuinely fixing a layout-settle issue the user described.

The tradeoff seems acceptable: worst case is a barely-visible one-frame correction hidden inside the 200ms drawer fade-in, so I'll include the rAF since it's cheap, bounded, and its cleanup properly cancels the frame if the effect re-runs. Now let me write it.

## Claude · 2026-08-26T10:32:04.986Z

Fixed. The root cause was ordering in the mobile drawer's scroll effect at `src/components/home/ConversationSidebar.tsx:894`.

**What was happening:** the effect checked the remembered scroll offset *first* and returned early if one existed, so the "center the selected run" branch below it was only ever reachable on the very first mobile open. Every later open restored a stale offset instead. That offset is saved when the drawer unmounts — normally right after you tap a session, with the tapped row sitting wherever your finger landed (typically above the middle). So reopening put you back near the old position rather than centered on the current session, and matching-by-luck explains the "works sometimes".

**Changes:**

- Centering now runs before the remembered-offset restore, and the offset is only a fallback (no selected row on screen, or desktop). `src/components/home/ConversationSidebar.tsx:906`
- Replaced `scrollIntoView({ block: "center" })` with an explicit offset written to the list viewport alone. `scrollIntoView` walks every scrollable ancestor, which on mobile also drags the drawer and document, and it measures the row while the drawer is still translated by its enter transition.
- The one-shot guard became `scrollSettledRef`: it stays unsettled while a mobile drawer's selected row hasn't rendered yet, so centering still fires when runs stream into the list late. Any real user scroll settles it, so nothing fights the finger — programmatic writes are distinguished from user scrolls by comparing against the last written offset.
- One `requestAnimationFrame` re-measure after the first paint, for layout that settles after mount. Backs off if the row detached or the user already scrolled.

**Tests:** the centering math moved to `src/components/home/conversation-sidebar-scroll.ts` and has real unit coverage in `tests/ui/conversation-sidebar-scroll.test.ts` (centering, clamping at both ends, short lists). `tests/ui/sidebar-layout.test.ts` gained an assertion that the restore path cannot precede centering.

`tsc --noEmit` is clean and `tests/ui/` passes except `tests/ui/terminal-fit.test.ts`, which fails on master too — pre-existing and unrelated to this change.

I have not driven this on a real mobile viewport; the verification is unit-level plus reading the effect ordering. If you want, I can run the app and check the drawer behavior directly.
